## 1. Legacy Wealth Blueprint client case studies

Stephanie Dailey saved over $20,000 in taxes in her first year

Summary: Stephanie and her husband are software engineers with five kids and over $300,000 in W-2 income, plus contract work from the consulting business they run. In her first year in the Legacy Wealth Blueprint she opened separate business bank accounts, started keeping real books, captured $27,000 in startup costs, deducted over $60,000 of bridge loan mortgage interest and put her kids on payroll through Gusto. She expects $40,000 to $50,000 over the following twelve months, and says the plan to pay off the house moved from about ten years to seven or eight.

Quote, Stephanie Dailey: "financially, we're high income on paper, but we also have real expenses, multiple income streams, and just a lot of moving pieces"
Quote, Stephanie Dailey: "it might have otherwise taken me years to get where we got in six months"
Quote, Stephanie Dailey: "we were just really blown away by how quickly it paid off"
Quote, Stephanie Dailey: "I would say, don't hesitate."

Interview: https://vimeo.com/1207907513 (22 min 47 sec)
Written case study: https://www.legacyinvestingshow.com/blog/stephanie-dailey-legacy-wealth-blueprint-case-study

Albert put his net worth growth at 25% and cut his home insurance in half

Summary: Albert is an ER physician in California who finished residency in 2021 with about $5,000 in a Roth and a combined tax bracket he puts at almost 50%. In the Legacy Wealth Blueprint he started tax loss harvesting, changed how he uses his HSA, opened a backdoor Roth IRA, took his portfolio off professional management and formed the entity for a new telemedicine clinic. He says his self-managed 401(k) is up about 19% year to date and his Roth about 40%.

Quote, Albert: "Before Legacy Wealth Blueprint, I would say the amount of financial knowledge I had was super slim."
Quote, Albert: "Since I live in California, my tax bracket is almost 50%, including federal and state."
Quote, Albert: "I had no idea about tax loss harvesting."
Quote, Albert: "I would say the information you provide is pretty priceless."

Interview: https://vimeo.com/1207906800 (26 min 4 sec)

Abigail calculated just under $100,000 in first-year ROI

Summary: Abigail left oil and gas in the summer of 2023 and runs the numbers for her own family and her parents. She finished the Legacy Wealth Blueprint modules inside the first month, rebuilt the entity and estate work she had set up badly eight years earlier, took a weekly coaching call with her coach Brandon, recategorized every transaction as business or not business, and went under contract on her first property. The just under $100,000 is her own calculation, run twice.

Quote, Abigail: "I have really more of the attitude that I really can't let another dollar go by that's not working for me."
Quote, Abigail: "a high yield savings account, the 4% at some point 5%, wasn't going to get me to freedom. That was just keeping me from drowning"
Quote, Abigail: "I'm coming in at just under a hundred thousand dollars ROI for just this year."
Quote, Abigail: "don't be afraid to be the person in the room who knows the least"

Interview: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5h9iwMgZxjk (36 min 25 sec)
Written case study: https://www.legacyinvestingshow.com/blog/2-deals-10000-month-profit

Gary makes $35,000 a month from seven Austin properties, three years in

Summary: Gary was a marketing executive at eBay and Chegg before building an Airbnb business in Austin, Texas. He runs seven properties with an eighth in progress, targets $10,000 gross and $5,000 to $6,000 net per property, and holds a 50% margin across the portfolio. He picks markets he would live in himself, treats pools and large guest capacity as the filter that removes competitors, and avoids month-long stays because discounted long guests demand repairs short guests never mention.

Quote, Gary: "One of my number one rules about Airbnb property management or investing is really trying to go into a place where I enjoy living at. Austin is one of those places I could totally see myself moving to."
Quote, Gary: "If I take away a pool, if I take away the jacuzzi, if I take away the 15 people capacity to only 10 people, now the filter basically puts me in competition with a lot more properties. So the property that has all these amenities becomes one of the few houses that has it."
Quote, Gary: "When I start lowering the prices, what I actually see is that the quality of the guest starts deteriorating. People that book in advance are higher quality because they're not procrastinators—they're really thinking about booking everything."
Quote, Gary: "There's many different ways to attack this problem or attack this industry. You really want to just pick the brains of people that are already doing it and share knowledge."

Michael locked up two Space Coast deals in one week and projects $12,000 a month

Summary: Michael is a serial entrepreneur who sold five companies before moving into Airbnb arbitrage on the Space Coast of Florida. He locked up two deals in one week, went from joining the program to a live listing in six weeks, and took his first booking five minutes after the listing went live. He reports a 60% close rate on landlord pitches, built around a service pitch aimed at travelling nurses and first responders, and imputes an 8% mortgage cost even on the property he owns outright.

Quote, Michael: "In five minutes it got listed and that's it. We're ocean view, two minute walk to the beach, rocket launches behind you. It literally got sucked up in seconds."
Quote, Michael: "I see a lot of people in the group just giving up and saying 'oh I called three or four and they turned me down.' Okay there's 20 others. Why aren't you making 20 calls? I would hunt the best areas, circle them, and just blast them."
Quote, Michael: "A lot of entrepreneurs and Airbnb people tend to leave that out. They say 'oh I own it so I don't pay a mortgage.' Yeah but you don't realize because you're not paying a mortgage that money should be making you some kind of money."
Quote, Michael: "How much would I ever have to pay or learn or go through trial and error to get to that point? For me to do what you gave me for your nominal amount would have cost me hundreds of thousands of dollars in headaches and mistakes."

Brian O'Leary makes $1,500 to $2,000 a month from one Iowa property, 30 days from joining to signed lease

Summary: Brian works full time in IT and added an Airbnb arbitrage unit in Iowa rather than buying another property. The four-bedroom historic home sleeps ten, went live in February and was cash flow positive by March at $1,500 to $2,000 a month after expenses. He landed the lease by borrowing the credibility of an existing operator team and their Superhost listings, and now runs it on one to two hours a week with automation.

Quote, Brian O'Leary: "I wanted to expand and instead of expanding into purchasing more properties, I decided to check out this arbitrage model. I'm using it to bring in more cash, and then properties that make sense, I'll see if there's an opportunity to switch from renting to owning."
Quote, Brian O'Leary: "That's where just having a group of people behind me was really helpful. I was able to tell her, 'Yes, while I am new to this, I am part of a team that has done this for dozens of homes and they are Superhosts. Here's a link to a few of those you can check them out. The level of quality photos and reviews you're seeing on these listings - that's what I'm going to bring to your property.'"
Quote, Brian O'Leary: "We started in this market because I wanted to start somewhat close to home. I really wanted to be there, to stage it, to see what things were looking like from beginning to end."
Quote, Brian O'Leary: "We've had guests that asked if there was a crib or a child gate and we just said yes, went out and bought it. This is a four bedroom house catered to families, so it's going to be good to have those things and be able to say yes to future guests."

Tom booked $25,000 in his first two weeks live on the Chesapeake Bay

Summary: Tom was a Capital One executive with a software engineering background and no rental experience when he launched his first Airbnb on the Chesapeake Bay in Maryland. In the first two weeks live he booked $25,000 through August and into September, and he now runs $5,000 to $6,000 a month in cash flow on 20 to 30 minutes a day. He built a three-person cleaning team for redundancy and chose a beach property he initially doubted, until his grandchildren showed him what guests would come for.

Quote, Tom: "These corporate gigs just suck the life out of you. I had to put this all on pause for a few months because we had a bunch of end of year activities that consumes everybody."
Quote, Tom: "Don't look at it like this horrible situation, this risky thing. Look at it like an adventure and keep your attitude kind of positive."
Quote, Tom: "To be honest with you, when I first saw it I was kind of like, 'I don't know, that's a beautiful piece of property but I'm not sure people are going to want to go here.'"
Quote, Tom: "My grandkids came down, they played on that beach all day and I'm like, 'That's a key, that is a key right there.'"

Kiana left a ten year finance career and makes $3,200 a month from two Scottsdale properties

Summary: Kiana spent more than a decade in finance and six months researching short-term rental rules across the country before acting. She now runs two properties ten minutes apart in Scottsdale, Arizona for $3,200 a month in combined cash flow, quit her job three months after joining, and has cut weekly management from ten hours to four. Her first property hit its conservative $2,500 a month benchmark, and when Airbnb demand softened she converted it to a one-year tenancy at the same number.

Quote, Kiana: "I've always believed in Financial Freedom. I also worked in finance for over a decade so I kind of have a little knowledge in that. I started researching this for like the last six months."
Quote, Kiana: "What do I have to lose and everything to gain? The overhead cost is not that much and the return is so high."
Quote, Kiana: "I had probably spent six months kind of just going around the whole entire United States kind of researching understanding short-term laws. I think that is kind of the higher risk on the sides of things so that was my biggest challenge."
Quote, Kiana: "We did the Airbnb for the first two months and then we actually were able to find a one-year tenant and so it still hit the same conservative Benchmark."

Lindsay Fischer and her husband booked $17,500 in 14 days live in the North Carolina mountains

Summary: Lindsay and her husband moved from nonprofit and 9-to-5 work into Airbnb arbitrage in the Western North Carolina mountains, thirty minutes outside Asheville. They went from first landlord contact to a signed contract in 21 days and secured the property on their fourth call. Fourteen days after going live on September 7 they had $17,500 booked across 14 reservations through New Year's Eve, with peak cash flow of $2,500 to $3,000 a month and about three hours of work a week.

Quote, Lindsay Fischer and her husband: "We just got married a year and a half ago so this was a new adventure even as a married couple. Finances can be a sticking point for couples, and it's just neat that we both hopped in on this together."
Quote, Lindsay Fischer and her husband: "We just knew we wanted to get on the market as soon as we could because the fall is peak season up here with the Western North Carolina mountains. So we were pressing them a little bit."
Quote, Lindsay Fischer and her husband: "We saw that on the AirDNA it showed that less than 20% of that area had the hot tub so we wanted to make sure we did that just to stand out."
Quote, Lindsay Fischer and her husband: "We named it something that people could remember so they'll come back to and we'll have repeat guests."

Hampton booked $20,000 in his first month on his parents' lakefront home

Summary: Hampton is a process engineer who started with his parents' lakefront property, showing them a projection of what the weekends they were not using could earn. The listing booked $20,000 within weeks and over $30,000 across four months. He has since added a West Virginia property returning 25 to 30% cash on cash, has a seven-bedroom lodge in progress, and manages the portfolio in about an hour a day using a local team he can message for on-site issues.

Quote, Hampton: "I actually took some of the tools that you kind of provided with us that were like a lot so far as breaking down what to expect and stuff and I took that and showed them. I said look this is what it's a great property, we love being up there. But here's what you could make if we rented it on some of the weekends we're not there."
Quote, Hampton: "Using that information we knew what we could do, we knew what we could use that to back into what we wanted to put into the little house to start with to get it up and running."
Quote, Hampton: "Having a good team or having a good list of people to call in that general area that can help out and go address these issues, and all I have to do is send a message to somebody be like hey can you do you mind stepping over here I need you to check this out."
Quote, Hampton: "Been extremely nervous about it but sometimes you just got to take a leap of faith and take the risk and it's definitely panning out."

Kirk hit 87% occupancy and $5,500 in March from one California property while nursing full time

Summary: Kirk is a full-time nurse in California who had never started a business before. He projected $2,000 a month conservatively and the property has run ahead of that, at $5,500 in March on 87% occupancy and over $4,400 in April on 70%. He dislikes phone calls, so he pitched owners and property managers face to face at the properties instead, and he set the listing apart from the neutral farmhouse look common in his area by using colour.

Quote, Kirk: "My ultimate goal is to spend more time with my family. I want the time and freedom to actually be here for them, see them grow up. That's why I'm doing this."
Quote, Kirk: "Initially I don't like talking on the phone. I try to prevent that as much as possible. But this really got my feet wet. In the beginning a lot of people were saying no, but I saw that as more of an incentive."
Quote, Kirk: "Instead of actually making the phone call, I would go see the property, talk to the owners, talk to the property manager, and pitch them in front of me."
Quote, Kirk: "Most of the people in my area have just the neutral color farmhouse type. So I tried to set us apart by putting a little bit more color, things to make it pop."

Executive Summary: Your Path to $50K+ Annual Tax Savings
This plan delivers $40,400 to $53,800 in first-year tax savings through eight strategic implementations, with an additional $7,200 in immediate cash flow improvement. By restructuring your business entities and employing family members strategically, you'll transform current family support expenses into legitimate business deductions while building systematic wealth through Bitcoin accumulation and real estate optimization.
Core Opportunity: Your IT consulting and AI business generates approximately $175K W-2 plus $20K side income, but you're currently operating without S-Corporation election and missing foundational strategies. The single highest-value action is filing Form 2553 for S-Corp election by March 15, 2026, saving $8,900-$11,900 annually in self-employment tax alone.
